3. **Codex** must run background checks against modified files to detect bugs, race conditions, or performance flaws.

## 3. Strict Project Rules
- Never modify this repository's upstream tracking references.
- Always append an execution log entry to this file after finishing a coding turn.
- **Never run `git reset`/`git stash` against the shared working tree for a "clean baseline"
  comparison (e.g. "stash my edits, measure something, pop the stash").** This repo has multiple
  concurrent sessions/agents working in the same checkout at once; a reset or a colliding stash-pop
  has already silently destroyed other sessions' uncommitted work more than once (2026-09-27/28 night
  session, see AUDIT_TRAIL.md around that date -- real work was lost and had to be redone twice
  before this rule was added). If a task needs a "before" comparison, build to a separate output
  directory and diff the results, or do the work in an isolated git worktree (`Agent` tool's
  `isolation: "worktree"`, or `git worktree add`) instead of touching the shared tree's index/stash.
  Commit real, verified work promptly rather than leaving it sitting uncommitted for long stretches --
  a stray reset elsewhere can't destroy a commit that already exists.
- **A plain branch does NOT isolate concurrent sessions sharing this checkout -- a worktree does.**
  `git checkout <branch>` changes what `HEAD` points to for the ENTIRE directory, so switching
  branches in the shared tree moves every other session's `HEAD` out from under it too (2026-10-01: one
  session's branch switch silently landed another session's commit on the wrong branch -- recovered via
  `git cherry-pick` into a separate worktree, nothing lost, but it should never have happened). If the
  owner asks for "a new branch" for a second concurrent task, treat that as a request for an isolated
  **worktree** (`git worktree add ../tau-alpha-<name> <branch>`, matching the already-established
  `tau-alpha-cymo` sibling-directory pattern, or the `Agent` tool's `isolation: "worktree"`) unless the
  prior session has already finished and the shared tree is free. Always run `git branch --show-current`
  immediately before any commit in the shared tree, not just `git status` for file changes -- a stray
  checkout can move `HEAD` without touching the working files at all, so file-level status alone won't
  catch it.
- **Whenever a Quartus fit is launched (`tools/vm_fit.py launch`), state an estimated duration and an
  estimated LOCAL finish clock time in the same message that reports the launch** -- not just "typical
  50 min-1h45m" as an abstract range, but "expect it done around HH:MM" computed from the actual launch
  time. Re-state this estimate (or correct it) whenever asked to check status. Base the estimate on this
  exact macro bundle's own prior fits where known (most of this project's recent history is the same
  `blit_g3_poly_blend_ram192_clk66_dbuf_lpc*` family, which has consistently landed near the top of the
  range, ~1h40-1h45m), falling back to the general range for an unfamiliar bundle.

## Subagent delegation policy

Before spawning a subagent (Task/Agent tool), check whether it's actually worth the overhead:
- Only delegate when the work is verbose/high-volume (test runs, log scanning, multi-file research,
  large doc fetches) or genuinely parallelizable. Small, quick lookups stay in the main context —
  a subagent's own startup cost (loading CLAUDE.md, skills, MCP tool defs) isn't worth it for trivial work.
- Never spawn a subagent just to "be thorough" — spawn one because its output would otherwise bloat
  the main conversation.

When delegating, write the subagent prompt to minimize its cost and the tokens it returns:
- Scope it tightly: state the exact question/deliverable, the specific files/paths/URLs it needs
  (not "explore the codebase"), and an explicit output format so it doesn't over-explain.
- Explicitly cap output: tell it to report back a short summary or the specific artifact needed —
  not full transcripts, full file contents, or step-by-step narration — since only the returned text
  re-enters the main context.
- Pick the cheapest model that can do the job: use `model: haiku` for mechanical/simple subagent tasks
  (formatting, running a command and reporting pass/fail, simple lookups), and reserve a stronger model
  for subagents doing real reasoning or judgment calls.
- Keep the spawn prompt itself short — everything in it enters the subagent's context immediately,
  on top of whatever CLAUDE.md/skills/MCP tools it auto-loads.
- Prefer one well-scoped subagent over several redundant ones; batch independent lookups into a single
  subagent call when they don't need separate context isolation.
- If a task doesn't clearly need isolation (verbose output, parallelism), just do it directly instead
  of delegating.

Font ROM repack's actual win is **not** confirmed by this step and cannot be -- synthesis reports declared content bits, unchanged by construction (4x8-bit lanes = the same 97,280 bits as one 32-bit array), so whether it reduces physical M10K block count is a fitter question the synthesis-only stage cannot answer, contrary to section 10's original "the block-count drop is the entire result" claim. - 2026-09-22 (Claude): Tau Omega (companion repo), docs only. Audited the companion engine against the owner's portability goal and recorded the result; committed there as `b596dba`. **Targets settled:** Pocket Sync (Tauri + Rust + TypeScript, AGPL-3.0) is the named integration target and the boundary is a plain Rust **crate dependency** -- no plugin ABI, C ABI, WASM or sidecar, because the host is Rust and, like every comparable manager, has no plugin system, so adoption means an upstream contribution or a fork and the real goal is being easy to vendor. Feishin was assessed on the owner's suggestion and **dropped** (Electron/GPL-3.0, streams from Navidrome/Jellyfin/Subsonic so it has no local files to index, no plugin API, no Rust runtime, little audience overlap); the source-abstraction work that target would have forced is explicitly not being done. Licence boundary written down (D-010): Tau Omega stays MIT OR Apache-2.0, our code may flow into their AGPL, theirs must never be copied back -- the same one-way rule this repo already lives under with GPL RTL (B-085). **Audit result:** the engine internals are sound and better than its own architecture doc asked for -- three dependencies (crc32fast, serde_json, sha2; no tokio/async/GUI), no `println!`/`process::Command`/`std::env`, no global or static state, no thread spawning, plan/execute is already a portable data contract rather than the UI flow the docs describe, and index parsing verifies the body CRC before any offset-driven traversal (the byte-level unwraps in the tag/cover parsers were each checked and are guarded by prior length checks, so not latent panics). All the gaps are at the boundary: the `/Assets/<platform>/common/` prefix rule is implemented separately in the CLI and the Tauri adapter (which also owns index-status presentation, contradicting their D-001); warnings and errors cross every boundary as English prose, with `TauError::code()` called only by the conformance test while 34 sites flatten to strings -- the tests prove the E-codes are right and the product discards them; and there are no progress or cancellation hooks at all despite the architecture doc promising cancellable jobs. Also corrected that doc where it described a codebase that does not exist (module tree, the ~20-crate dependency list vs the actual three, the unbuilt cancellation claim, five extension points that exist only on paper) -- it matters because their plan drives a code-generating assistant from those documents. Fail-safe came free: old RTL's 2-bit opcode read truncates the new op value to RUN, not a hang. - 2026-09-22 (Claude): RTL/sim, no hardware. B-104: owner said "continue with B2 and B6" -- both built on B1's OP_BLIT/R_BLT_IDX/R_BLT_DATA foundation. B6 (OP_BAR, meter column): two chained RECT fills, no new burst mechanism -- the existing rect_active/A_WRWAIT loop runs twice per command, second segment queued and re-armed the instant the first's last row retires. cmd_addr = span top-left (existing convention), cmd_glyph (unused outside CHAR) = lit-row count clamped to height, cmd_fg/cmd_bg = lit/unlit colours (COPY's "no other use" precedent). Decided (not left implicit) that lit rows are the bottom of the span, documented in code. B2 (colour-key transparency): needed more than "one comparator" to be correct -- showing dest through a keyed pixel needs a dest READ, which OP_BLIT/OP_COPY never did (write-only). Added a real destination pre-read phase (new state A_KEYDST, a clone of the existing A_COPYRD read-into-glyphbuf loop but unconditional); the source read then just skips writing glyphbuf for a KEY-matching word, leaving the pre-read dest pixel in place -- no separate select/blend stage. OP_COPY stays untouched, never keys. New sticky field 5 (R_BLT_IDX=4: enable+colour); blt_idx widened 2->3 bits, wraps 4->0 for a clean 5-write burst load. Verified in sim/tb_mp3_fb.v: BAR (split/fully-lit/fully-unlit cases, exact addresses and colours per segment); B2 (one KEY-matching word of four keeps dest, the other three and a non-matching row take src normally, plus keying-disabled control case). Two new mutation parameters (BUG_IGNORE_KEY joins B-103's BUG_IGNORE_BLIT_STRIDE) both confirmed caught by make test-rtl-fb-mutation. make rtl-lint/test-host/test-rtl all pass, 0 failures. - 2026-09-22 (Claude): RTL/sim, no hardware. B-105: owner said "continue with B3 and B4." B3: analysed rather than built -- the Amiga barrel-shifter/word-mask mechanism section 5 describes is for bit-packed multi-pixel-per-word formats, and this engine is one pixel per SDRAM word, so the literal mechanism has nothing to translate to; what it would buy for a blit, B1's own generalised addressing already provides for free. The real gap (CHAR sub-glyph clipping, the marquee's actual limitation) needs a firmware change first -- checked fw/player.c and found fb_char() never writes R_FB_SIZE at all, so retrofitting cmd_w/cmd_h as CHAR clip fields would silently feed garbage leftover RECT/COPY dimensions into every existing glyph draw. Confirmed by reading the source, not assumed; recorded rather than built. B4 (OP_SBLIT, scaled blit): a brand-new opcode, so none of B3's compatibility risk applies. Reuses CHAR's own Bresenham registers directly (never both active at once) against a variable source size (cmd_w/cmd_h, safe here) instead of CHAR's fixed 16px cell; new sblit_ext() computes the output extent via a small multiply-by-constant, no divider. Every output pixel issues its own single-word SDRAM read (new state A_SBLIT), returning through A_IDLE between pixels like every other transaction -- scanout can preempt between any two words. Destination steps by the sticky stride every row (reusing B1's blit_dst_addr unchanged); source steps only when the Y-Bresenham condition fires. Same 127-word glyphbuf limit as OP_COPY/OP_BLIT. Verified in sim/tb_mp3_fb.v: 1x (agrees with the row-burst path pixel-for-pixel) and 2x (a 2x1 source doubles to 4x2 output, hand-computed values matched exactly on the first run). New mutation parameter BUG_SBLIT_NO_SCALE (silently drops to an unscaled copy) confirmed caught. make rtl-lint/test-host/test-rtl all pass, 0 failures (3 mutation cases total for this file now). Owner reloaded `TAU_0_5_0_A_12` through the Pocket's own core menu (with B-189's corrected firmware) and reproduced the hang; found a second real process gap doing the re-read -- loading a core through the Pocket's UI reprograms the FPGA fresh from the SD card's own (pre-ISSP) `.rbf`, silently overwriting the JTAG-loaded DBGM/IFPS debug bitstream (`get_service_paths issp` came back empty). Reloaded the ISSP `.sof` via `quartus_pgm -m jtag`, had the owner reproduce the hang again with plain buttons only. Found and fixed a second real tool bug: `issp_read_probe_data -instance $path` (used in this session's later scripts) silently returns the literal string `"error"` with no exception -- the correct form, confirmed via `help issp_read_probe_data`, is positional (`issp_read_probe_data $path`). Clean result, 12 reads over ~2.4s: **`DBGM = 0x00` constant** (now real evidence -- `bt_begin()`'s first line genuinely never executes), **`IFPS = 0x44` constant** (`gi` and `i_is_psram` set, every live-transaction bit -- `busy`/`ictl_req`/`dctl_req`/`iCYC`/`iSTB` -- zero), `BLIT` matching B-186's idle/fill pattern. This weakens rather than confirms B-188's PSRAM-fetch-stall hypothesis: an actively-stalled fetch would show a held request, not an idle arbiter. The more consistent picture: something upstream of the `bt_begin()` call site, in ordinary on-chip code, holds the CPU first, with no instruction-fetch bus activity at all while stuck. Next step is the already-committed PCAD probe (`iADR`/`dADR`, `3527cde`) -- needs its own Quartus fit, not yet launched. docs/AUDIT_TRAIL.md B-190, docs/CURRENT_STATUS.md, docs/JTAG_DEBUG_ACCESS.md section 6 updated with both tool/process gaps.
- 2026-09-25 (Claude): MILESTONE (B-191). Built and fit the PCAD probe (0 negative slack, 0 errors), loaded via JTAG, owner reproduced the hang, read all four probes. `iCYC=0`/`iSTB=0` (idle instruction fetch, matches B-190) but `dCYC=1`/`dSTB=1`/`dACK=0` -- a genuinely live, permanently unacknowledged DATA transaction. `iADR` sits inside `bt_draw()`; disassembling that exact PC found the CPU frozen on `lhu s0,964(a5) # a00003c4` -- `bt_crumb_read()`'s own SDRAM halfword readback (964 = BT_CRUMB_ADDR<<1), called from `bt_draw()`'s idle-screen redraw, before the user's Start press ever reaches `bt_begin()`. This is the actual root cause of the whole B-166..B-190 hang investigation: the checkpoint mechanism B-176 built specifically to safely observe a hang, without risk of hanging itself, is the thing that hangs. Leading hypothesis for why (unconfirmed): `blit_probe()` uses the identical raw-pointer SDRAM-window access and works, but only after `blit_probe_ensure()` has fired at least once elsewhere (`bt_begin()` or a meter-preview thumbnail draw in Settings) -- `bt_crumb_read()` may be hitting this window completely cold, first-access-of-boot. Proposed a free, zero-engineering test to the owner (visit a meter-preview screen first, then Blit Test) before any firmware change. docs/AUDIT_TRAIL.md B-191, docs/CURRENT_STATUS.md updated.

All three alternative theories ruled out. Root cause fully confirmed: `0xA0000000`-relative raw CPU pointers hang unconditionally on every firmware/bitstream combination; `PL_SDRAM_BASE`(`0xA0100000`)-relative ones work every time. Real, pre-existing, unconditional bug, not caused by anything built this session. docs/AUDIT_TRAIL.md B-192.

Root cause confirmed by reading the actual video-timing RTL: scanout reads are pipelined one row ahead (`do_fill`/`linebuf`) but CPU writes have zero frame synchronization at all -- a real, structural gap, not an opcode-choice problem. Found two unused hooks (a dead `vblank` input at core_top, an internal `vs_pulse` CDC-able the same way B7's SDRAM busy counter already is) and a concrete high-value opportunity: `fb_round_rect`/`fb_round_rect_on` (used for nearly every panel/selected-row in the whole UI) is pure software issuing ~33 separate transactions per call; B11's `OP_RRECT` (just timing-fixed, B-231) does the same shape in one hardware command. Wrote a phased design into `docs/PHASE_F_SPEC.md` section 15 (T0: CDC vblank into an MMIO register; T1: convert `fb_round_rect*` to `OP_RRECT` + a real vblank-aware draw-batching layer, the actual "UI controller"; T2: true double buffering, held -- SDRAM capacity isn't the blocker, RTL/convention complexity is) and cross-referenced from `docs/ARCHITECTURE_ROADMAP.md`'s UI/UX redesign placeholder (B-115). No RTL/firmware built, design only. The Winamp Bars/Scope bug investigation from earlier this session (preset-switch invisibility, non-reactive Configure preview, garbled Scope preview, per-mode settings independence) is paused mid-fix at the owner's explicit redirect to this task -- root causes already understood, to be written up and fixed next turn. Full detail: docs/AUDIT_TRAIL.md B-232.

- 2026-09-25 (Claude): docs (B-237). Owner asked for a lightweight, high-performance UI/graphics library making full use of the blit engine, prior-art investigated first; named the library Helios and the blit engine Talos. - 2026-09-25 (Claude): docs (B-237 addendum, colour/theming). Owner asked how colour works, palette vs true-colour, Figma colour-variable linking, alpha/blend modes. Confirmed by reading the RTL/firmware: framebuffer is true-colour RGB565 throughout, not indexed -- the CLUT (B8) is a separate 256-entry lookup for palette-indexed source bitmaps only (sprite-palette pattern). Theme colours today are a fixed swatch list. A Figma export is plausible via the same offline pattern as fonts/icons, with two real costs found: lossy RGB565 quantization needs reporting not silent rounding, and the text-AA gamma table is fitted to the current palette/background specifically, needing re-verification per new theme. No per-pixel alpha channel exists anywhere (RGB565 has no spare bits); text glyphs already blend per-pixel via coverage (real, text-only), B5 (shelved, timing-cliff never fixed) is one uniform alpha/ratio per whole blit command, nothing like Figma's blend-mode list -- a uniform-opacity layer maps directly once un-shelved, a per-pixel alpha gradient must be pre-flattened at export time instead. docs/HELIOS_SPEC.md section 7.4. Design only. Full detail: docs/AUDIT_TRAIL.md B-237.

- 2026-09-25 (Claude): docs (B-241). Started scoping B13 (gradient bar) and found, by reading OP_BAR's actual RTL rather than the spec summary, that it's not a row-by-row iterator -- it's two stacked whole-segment bursts, which is why it's cheap. A genuine per-row gradient needs one burst per row instead, potentially costing MORE transactions than the software sequence it was meant to replace, not fewer. Owner: "let's hold this to the end of Talos/Helios" -- held, docs/HELIOS_SPEC.md section 6 and docs/PHASE_F_SPEC.md's B13 row updated, no RTL written. Full detail: docs/AUDIT_TRAIL.md B-241.

- 2026-09-25 (Claude): docs (B-237 addendum). Owner asked about loadable fonts at fixed scales for sharpness, and whether icons should be SVG-rasterized on-device or handled like fonts. Found a real gap while answering: today's "scaled" text (TS_1X..TS_3X) rides OP_SBLIT's Bresenham stepping, not separate bitmaps per size -- crisp at integer factors (2x/3x), not at 1.5x. Design response: loaded fonts get zero runtime scaling ever, each size is its own offline-rasterized atlas, same offline-tooling pattern as font_rom.v/the AA gamma table/meter thumbnails. Icons: also offline-rasterized (no on-device SVG parsing -- a vector rasterizer has no business on a float-less core competing with audio), specializing the already-proven B8 meter-thumbnail pipeline rather than inventing a new one; found a real connection to B9 (palette re-index) for theming icons with one base bitmap + palette swap. - 2026-09-25 (Claude): firmware (B-234). Resumed the paused TAU_DEV_50 review. Found and fixed four real bugs: (1) bars invisible after a preset change -- the per-band redraw-skip cache had a documented "0xFF = force redraw" convention that nothing ever actually used; (2) Configure preview not audio-reactive -- every Settings page only redraws on an input key edge, wrong for a page whose whole point is a continuously-animating live meter; (3) Scope preview garbled -- it unconditionally called `ui_bg_restore()`, which reads a gradient cache pre-rendered only for the player screen's own row range, garbage when called from Configure's different position; (4) settings not independent per mode -- one shared `wviz_cfg_t`/preset table/CUSTOM tracker held both Bars-only and Scope-only fields. Fixed all four: split into `wviz_bars_cfg_t`/`wviz_scope_cfg_t` with independent presets/CUSTOM state; a new `wviz_force` flag (also wired into the pre-existing player-screen X-cycle handler, which had the same omission for these newer arrays) forces a clean repaint on any context change; `wviz_scope_tick()` gained a `use_gradient`/`bg` param mirroring bars' own convention; the main loop now forces continuous redraw while the Configure page is open; the row list is now filtered per mode. Every firmware build target rebuilds clean, `make test-host` passes (22/22), the QR export wire format is unchanged. Not committed, not yet on hardware. Fifth issue (spectrum/VIZ_LED freezing) diagnosed but not fixed: `meter_afford()`'s wide GO/STOP hysteresis could latch permanently under contention this project didn't have when it was ported -- a plausible, unconfirmed mechanism, recommended a cheap counter to verify before touching the thresholds. - 2026-09-26 (Claude): firmware/card. B-296..B-299: alpha.23 corrupted on real hardware (Winamp Oscilloscope, whole-frame tearing, audio jitter); reverted the card to alpha.22 (hit and recovered from a real card disconnect mid-backup, no data lost). Root-caused as firmware, not RTL/timing: `wviz_scope_tick()`'s hardware-wave-path clamp on `lo`/`hi` was one-sided each, letting a DC-biased capture window escape the meter's box and cast a negative coordinate to a huge `uint32_t` draw address. Fixed (both bounds, both variables, plus a defensive signed-space clamp at the draw site) in both the hardware and software scope paths. Packaged the fix on alpha.23's exact bitstream (seed 2) as `alfatreze.TAU_0_5_0_A_25`, installed. Audio jitter persisted after the fix -- traced to a real cost regression, not a residual bug: the hardware wave path draws up to 256 columns x up to 3 `fb_rect()` calls, ~21x `wviz_bars_tick()`'s own baseline (`DRAW STALL` read 34,663 ms cumulative, `CPU LOAD` 100%).

"Radio button rounded issue still present" despite `8d529d1`'s corner-cut LUT: a real race, not a LUT-content bug -- `fb_round_rect_on()` rewrote the shared 16-entry `rc_cut_lut_r` register file via `rc_lut_ensure()` without waiting for a still-in-flight prior `OP_RRECT` at a different radius to finish (`fb_rrect()` only waits for what came before it, not for itself); `set_disc()`'s 2-3 back-to-back different-radius calls per row is the trigger. Fixed by moving `fb_wait()` before the LUT rewrite. make test-host passes; release/player-library-diagnostic-profile rebuild clean. If it reads a value tracking the configured trail %, the bug is hardware not applying it (needs the JTAG probe extended, a real RTL/fit cycle); if it reads 0 or doesn't track trail, the bug is firmware's own computation -- much cheaper. make test-host passes, heap-gap/cold-call checks pass; packaged as alfatreze.TAU_0_6_0_A_33 on the same glyphbuf-t200 bitstream, install pending (card unmounted). - 2026-09-30 (Claude): RTL (B-441). Owner's ALPHA readings on A_33 conclusively nailed it down: 153/243/51 at trail 40/5/80% are exact matches for (100-trail)*256/100, identical in both the normal and accumulating states -- firmware sends the right, different value every time, but the visible result never changes regardless. That means the value firmware writes and the value blend_ch() computes with aren't the same thing -- narrows the remaining cause to the sticky field's own hardware storage not latching what's written. - 2026-09-30 (Claude): JTAG + firmware (B-444). Loaded the extended probe via JTAG, owner reproduced the accumulation at the default 40% trail: BLND reads ALPHA=153 MODE=0, an exact match for firmware's own value -- rules out the sticky-field hardware-latch theory too. Every register-level check this investigation has built now reads correct. Worked out why JTAG polling can never resolve this further: blend_ch()'s >>8 approximation makes a 1-LSB-diff pixel pair blend-indistinguishable for any alpha (proven by the arithmetic), and a genuinely fading pixel converges within ~4-5 frames -- far faster than any opportunistic poll -- so every live sample was always going to land on an already-converged pixel regardless of whether hardware is healthy. Built the real follow-up instead: dbg_pixel_log() logs one fixed on-screen pixel's actual content via the existing mailbox into a 4-entry ring buffer, surfaced on a new PIXHIST Info row -- a real frame-by-frame decay trace, no JTAG needed. Caught and fixed a real self-inflicted bug along the way (a comment edit that left stray text outside its closing */, caught by the real firmware build, not just make test-host) and added a second host-harness stub. make test-host clean, heap-gap/cold-call checks pass, and this time the real firmware build was also confirmed clean. Full detail: docs/AUDIT_TRAIL.md B-444.
- 2026-09-30 (Claude): firmware, hardware-confirmed bug fix (B-445). Owner hit a real, reproducible crash: scrolling to PIXHIST crashed the core the instant it rendered, on real silicon, every time. Root cause: set_draw_ro()'s fixed char v[40] row buffer has no bounds check, and PIXHIST's 4-entry design wrote 45 bytes into it -- a genuine stack overflow. Fixed by reducing to 3 entries (35 chars, comfortably under 40), matching STRIP's own convention. make test-host, heap-gap/cold-call checks, and the real firmware build all confirmed clean. - 2026-09-30 (Claude): tooling, hardware-confirmed bug fix (B-448). Owner hit a total black-screen boot on alpha.36. Every file-level check looked clean, but testing build determinism directly found the real cause: `tools/check_heap_gap.py` rebuilds tracked targets with no env flags, and `player-library-diagnostic-profile`'s output path isn't redirected by RAM_192K/etc the way `release`'s is -- so a manually-flagged build and check_heap_gap.py's unflagged rebuild of the same nominal target land at the identical file path, and this project's own established verify-then-package workflow (build, then run checks, then package) silently swapped in the wrong variant with no error anywhere. Fixed at the tool level: new `--build-flags` on `tools/package_dev_build.py` builds the firmware itself as the literal last step before packaging, eliminating the risk by construction. Rebuilt clean, packaged as alfatreze.TAU_0_6_0_A_37, owner confirmed it boots fine. Full detail: docs/AUDIT_TRAIL.md B-448.

- 2026-09-30 (Claude): firmware, real root cause found and fixed (B-450). Owner reproduced the accumulation and read PIXHIST at its B-449 location: 10A2/10A2 x3, unchanged across all 3 frames, against expected W18C3 -- a real multi-LSB gap, not another 1-LSB-indistinguishable dead end (KB-078), the first decisive evidence of a genuine hardware-adjacent bug. Read src/fpga/core/mp3_fb.sv directly (not more register probing) and found the module's own documented contract: H2's automatic per-buffer offsetting (dbuf_addr(), keyed on dbuf_cpu_buf) covers ONLY RECT/CHAR/COPY dispatch; every true BLIT-mode opcode (BLIT/BAR/SBLIT/CBLIT/RRECT) is addressed purely through firmware-set sticky SRC_BASE/DST_BASE and is untouched by dbuf_cpu_buf -- confirmed at the exact RTL line (blit_dst_addr <= blt_dst_base + q_addr, no dbuf_addr() call). wviz_scope_tick()'s trail fade uses fb_blit() (BLIT-mode) and never called fb_set_bases(), so it always faded buffer 0 regardless of which buffer dbuf_redraw_begin()/dbuf_redraw_end() (B-397) had since made the real CPU target -- exactly the owner's reported trigger (Settings/fullscreen/Configure open-close) -- while the RECT-class trace bars correctly followed R_DBUF_CPU onto the displayed buffer. Same bug class already found and fixed once for Chladni (fw/chladni.inc, B-414), a second independent instance. Fixed in fw/player.c: new ui_bg_cur_buf() helper + ui_bg_buf tracking (forces the lazy gradient-strip rebuild whenever the active H2 buffer changes -- its rows sit inside V_ACT so, unlike Chladni's CHL_PLANE_Y scratch, it genuinely has a separate physical copy per buffer); ui_bg_blend() now brackets its fb_blit() calls with fb_set_bases()/restore-to-(0,0), matching the Chladni/Settings-crossfade precedent; the STRIP and PIXHIST diagnostics made buffer-aware too so they stay meaningful. No RTL change, no new Quartus fit -- same glyphbuf-t200 bitstream. make test-host, heap-gap/cold-call checks, and real firmware builds (release/player-library-diagnostic/player-library-diagnostic-profile) all clean. Packaged with tools/package_dev_build.py --build-flags (closing B-448's build-clobber risk) as alfatreze.TAU_0_6_0_A_39, RBF hash confirmed, installed via tools/install_dev_core.py (A_38 backed up and removed, media/library carried, caches cleared). - 2026-09-30 (Claude): firmware, real root cause found and fixed (B-452). Owner ran the H2 closure test list: 12/13 passed, only Configure-page meter cycling failed -- Chladni doesn't render, Bars/VU Master static, only Winamp Scope plays. Owner confirmed this is scoped exactly to the Configure page, same meters fine everywhere else. Root cause found from source: ui_draw_dynamic_cold()'s once-per-frame publish step (peak_l/peak_r/spec_lvl[], fed from meters_feed()'s always-on accumulation) is gated behind UI_OVERLAY_UP -- correct for the normal player screen, but Settings being open for the Configure preview is itself a UI_OVERLAY_UP case, so Bars/Chladni/VU Master froze at whatever data they had the instant Settings opened. Winamp Scope was immune because it manages its own R_WAVE_CTL capture directly, independent of this publish step -- exactly why it kept working. B-415 only fixed the drawing gate, never this data gate. Fixed: extracted the publish block into COLD_FN3 meters_publish() (fw/player.c), called from its original site plus wvcfg_preview_tick() (fw/settingsui.inc), deliberately bypassing the overlay gate for the meter being previewed. First build attempt dropped heap gap ~820B across all targets (caught by check_heap_gap.py, not inspection) -- the extracted function needed its own COLD_FN3 to land back in the cold image; fixed, heap gap restored to exact baseline. make test-host, heap-gap, cold-call checks, and all three firmware targets clean. Packaged as alfatreze.TAU_0_6_0_A_40, RBF hash confirmed, installed via tools/install_dev_core.py. Full detail: docs/AUDIT_TRAIL.md B-452.
- 2026-09-30 (Claude): firmware, real root cause found and fixed (B-453). Owner retested alfatreze.TAU_0_6_0_A_40 after B-452: Bars/Scope/VU Master now animate correctly in the Configure preview, but Chladni still doesn't render at all there. Root cause: chladni_tick_box() (fw/chladni.inc) has its own hard-coded `if (chl_ok == 2u || UI_OVERLAY_UP) return 0;` -- a raw UI_OVERLAY_UP check, stricter than every other meter's draw primitives, and unlike them never deferred to the caller's ov_draw override. Every other draw call checks FB_HELD() (`(UI_OVERLAY_UP || ui_fullscreen) && !ov_draw`), which is exactly what lets wvcfg_preview_tick()'s ov_draw=1 bracket (B-415's own mechanism) through for the other three meters. Fixed by swapping the raw check for FB_HELD(). Also fixed a host-test gap: sim/chladni_module_harness.c had no FB_HELD() stub, only UI_OVERLAY_UP -- added one. make test-host/heap-gap/cold-call checks all clean, no cold-code regression this time. - 2026-09-30 (Claude): RTL/firmware, real fix (B-454). Owner asked to move on with the real RTL fix for the Winamp Bars fullscreen clamp (B-406's 127-row clamp is only a mitigation -- OP_BAR's lit-row count is physically 7 bits wide on the chip, so no firmware change could let bars grow past it, and FS_FIG_H=323 genuinely needs more). Widened it to 9 bits in RTL: a NEW, separate 2-bit field cmd_glyph_hi (BAR-only; CHAR's 7-bit ASCII and RRECT's radius keep reading the original field unchanged), claiming 2 of the FIFO word's 4 spare padding bits (mp3_fb.sv, CW stays 88, every other field's bit position unchanged) and 2 of R_FB_GO's genuinely-unused bits 15-16 (mp3_soc.v), wired through core_game.vh. New mutation hook BUG_IGNORE_BAR_HI. Firmware: bar_hi_ready/BAR_WIDE_READY() forward-declared directly above fb_bar()'s own definition (~1,460 lines before blit_probe.inc's #include, unlike every other probe caller) so it can probe on first actual need; bar_hi_probe() (fw/blit_probe.inc) is a genuine new hardware self-test (OP_BAR itself has existed since B-104, only the extra bits are in question) -- draws a real 2-row bar with lit=128 and reads back row 1's colour to tell old hardware (bg, low 7 bits truncate to 0) from new (fg, clamps to h=2) directly. Old/unproven hardware keeps the exact 127-row clamp it always had -- safe, no regression. sim/tb_mp3_fb.v gained a dedicated push_bar() task (every existing BAR test untouched) and a real h=250/lit=200 test case (both values impossible in the old 7-bit field). make rtl-lint, test-rtl-fb, test-rtl-fb-mutation (6/6 mutants killed), full test-rtl, and test-host all pass. check_heap_gap.py's ~800B drop across all targets is real and expected (genuinely new hot code, matching blit_probe()/rrect_probe()'s own precedent) -- baseline updated. Launched the real fit (bar-hi-b454, the exact glyphbuf-t200 bundle, both seeds), staged tree confirmed via SSH grep. Result pending. Not committed. Full detail: docs/AUDIT_TRAIL.md B-454.

Wired the actual production audio path per section 14/K2's own guidance -- `pcm_fifo.v` itself UNTOUCHED (its proven priming/glide/flush logic stays exactly as-is), the resampler wired as a second parallel consumer of the existing `fifo_l`/`fifo_r`/`pcm_sample_tick` signals the spectrum/wave-meter taps already read. `eq_biquad.v` gained one new output port, `tick_out`, mirroring its own existing internal 48kHz tick (purely additive, re-confirmed bit-exact against `tools/eq_model.py`). `mp3_soc.v` gained a new STICKY `cymo_live_en` bit (R_CYMO_CTRL bit 2, explicitly added to the `if(rst)` block since the pulse-reset preamble doesn't cover sticky registers -- a real correctness requirement, easy to miss) defaulting to 0 (byte-identical-to-today audio path) until firmware opts in after self-testing, same probe-then-adopt convention as POLY_FW/LPC_FW/BLIT_READY(); while live, push/start run fully automatically from hardware (pcm_sample_tick / the EQ's own tick), zero CPU involvement. Full `make test-rtl` (44 PASSED, all mutations still caught) and `make test-host` both green. Owner asked whether this needs a new fit: yes -- the already-collected `cymo-b472` RBF (B-473/B-475) predates this wiring entirely, so nothing about this specific configuration is proven on real hardware yet, though the added logic is small and off an already-generous timing margin. Not yet done: the new fit itself, a firmware probe (`CYMO_RESAMP_READY()`), a Diagnostics-only live-enable toggle, card install, hardware A/B. Full detail: docs/AUDIT_TRAIL.md B-476.

- 2026-10-01 (Claude): RTL (B-484 MILESTONE). First genuine hardware-confirmed bug in the Cymo arc. Owner tested the live resampler path on real hardware (MacCunn FLAC/MP3, Clementi, all 44.1kHz) and heard a clear, reproducible "vibrato"-like artifact on every track. Root-caused numerically before touching RTL: B-476's design reused `eq_biquad.v`'s own internal 48kHz tick for the resampler's `start` signal, but that tick was never built to be accurate (fine for an EQ's IIR coefficients, not fine for a sample-rate converter) -- under `TAU_CLK66`, `66,666,667/48,000` truncates to 1388 instead of 1388.89, producing a real 48,030.74 Hz tick instead of 48,000. Through the resampler's fixed 147:160 ratio this implies an effective 44,128 Hz input-consumption rate against `pcm_fifo`'s correctly-calibrated 44,100.00 Hz supply -- a ~28 Hz beat causing the resampler to run ahead of its own input and re-shift a stale sample roughly every 35ms, squarely matching the reported symptom. Fixed by giving the resampler its own dedicated fractional-accumulator tick (the exact technique `pcm_fifo.v` already uses correctly, reusing the SAME `pcm_rate` reset-default constants, independently re-derived and confirmed: 3092376→47,999.993 Hz, 3435974→48,000.002 Hz). `eq_biquad.v`'s now-dead `tick_out` port (B-476) fully reverted -- the module is back to its exact pre-B-476 form, re-confirmed bit-exact against `tools/eq_model.py`. Full `make test-rtl` (44 PASSED, all mutations caught) and `make test-host` both green. Not yet done: synthesis check, a real fit, package+install, and the hardware re-test that would actually confirm the fix -- the math matches the reported symptom closely but remains a hypothesis until re-tested, per this project's own discipline. Full detail: docs/AUDIT_TRAIL.md B-484.
- 2026-10-01 (Claude): RTL (B-488). Second hardware-confirmed Cymo bug. Owner's re-test of B-486's tick fix: music sounded better overall but with "a tiny distortion, kind of like a constant noise"; a 1kHz tone test was decisive -- toggling LIVE on repeatedly gave a clearly DIFFERENT pitch each time, OFF always constant. Reasoned from first principles: a fixed-rational-ratio resampler's long-term output frequency cannot depend on starting phase, so a per-toggle pitch difference has to be a settling transient mistaken for steady pitch, not an actual ratio error (already fixed/re-verified in B-484/B-486). Found the real gap: `cymo_live_en`'s clear connection only reset on an explicit MMIO clear or a real track change, never on the toggle itself -- every engage resumed against stale 32-tap history from wherever it was last live. Fixed with a plain rising-edge detector OR'd into the clear signal, forcing a fresh reset on every toggle-on; deliberately left the B-486 tick accumulator untouched (must stay pristine/continuous). Full `make test-rtl` (44 PASSED) and `make test-host` both green. Honestly flagged: the separately-reported "tiny constant noise" during ordinary playback is NOT confirmed fixed by this -- could be the same transient if it coincides with track changes, or a separate open issue (inherent push/pop timing jitter between two independent accumulators, not yet investigated). Full detail: docs/AUDIT_TRAIL.md B-488.
- 2026-10-01 (Claude): RTL/firmware (B-492/B-495). Resumed Cymo per the prior session's own handoff: owner re-tested B-488's toggle-reset fix from a cold boot -- both symptoms (per-toggle pitch variance, constant low-level noise) unchanged, at most placebo-level. Read the live-path wiring rather than guess a third time and found the real gap: `cymo_auto_we` pushed into the resampler on EVERY `pcm_fifo` tick unconditionally, never checking `pop_req` at all, despite the module's own header documenting the contract as "push exactly once per asserted pop_req." Two free-running ticks (pcm_fifo's track-rate tick, Cymo's own fixed 48kHz tick) with no handshake -- explains both symptoms, and explains why B-488 (which only resets the resampler's OWN state) could never have fixed it. Fixed by gating `cymo_auto_we` on `cymo_pop_req`; added `R_CYMO_DIAG` (MMIO 0x160), a saturating stale-consume counter surfaced on the Info page, for direct evidence instead of listening alone a third time. `make rtl-lint`/`test-rtl`/`test-host` all pass (caught and fixed a real Icarus declare-after-use ordering bug along the way, same class as B-103). Fit `cymo-b492` launched 22:12 WEST (~23:52-23:57 WEST estimate). Mid-wait, built the owner's Start+X chord request (jump straight to Meter > Configure, `fw/settingsui.inc`/`fw/player.c`) -- gated off while the library browser is open since it already binds X to a favourites toggle there. Hit a real shared-checkout hazard committing it: another concurrent session ran a branch checkout in this SAME directory between my two commits, so the Start+X commit landed on their branch instead of `main` -- recovered cleanly via a separate `git worktree` + `cherry-pick`, nothing lost, but also found and fixed a genuine duplicate audit-ID collision (both sessions had independently used B-494) by renumbering mine to B-495. Added a new standing rule (section 3) that a plain branch does not isolate concurrent sessions sharing this checkout, only a worktree does, and that `git branch --show-current` must be checked immediately before every commit in the shared tree, not just file-level `git status`. Full detail: docs/AUDIT_TRAIL.md B-492/B-493/B-495.
